Part III
Important preliminary results for
Theorem

1

Appendix SM1
An oracle inequality for the likelihood

Recall that for some fixed
\Delta_{n},M_{n}
, we define
A_{n}=\left\{{\lVert\hat{\eta}-\eta_{0}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{n},\overline{Z}_{n}\leq M_{n}}\right\}
in (
A.1
). In this section, we bound

\operatorname{P}\left[{A_{n},\mathrm{Sub}_{n}(\hat{G}_{n})\gtrsim_{\mathcal{H}}\epsilon_{n}}\right],

where we recall
\mathrm{Sub}_{n}
from (
A.1
), for some rate function

\epsilon_{n}
. It is convenient to state a set of high-level assumptions on the rates

\Delta_{n},M_{n}
. These are satisfied for the choice (
OA1
).

Assumption SM1.1
.

Assume that

(1)

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\Delta_{n}\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\frac{1}{M_{n}^{3}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}1
, and

(2)

\sqrt{\log n}\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}M_{n}
.

Our main result in this section is the following oracle inequality.

Theorem SM1.1
.

Let
\lVert\hat{\eta}-\eta_{0}\rVert_{\infty}=\max(\lVert\hat{m}-m_{0}\rVert_{\infty},\lVert\hat{s}-s_{0}\rVert_{\infty})
and

\overline{Z}_{n}=\max_{i\in[n]}|Z_{i}|\vee 1
. Suppose
\hat{G}_{n}
satisfies
1
.
Under
2
,
4
,
3
, and
SM1.1
, there exists
constants
C_{1,\mathcal{H}},C_{2,\mathcal{H}}>0
such that the following tail bound
holds: Let

(SM1.1)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{n}

\displaystyle=M_{n}\sqrt{\log n}\Delta_{n}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h\left({f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}},f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}}}\right)+\Delta_{n}M_{n}\sqrt{\log n}e^{-C_{2,\mathcal{H}}M_{n}^{\alpha}}+\Delta_{n}^{2}M_{n}^{2}\log n+M_{n}^{2}\frac{\Delta_{n}^{1-\frac{1}{2p}}}{\sqrt{n}}.

Then,

\operatorname{P}\left[{\overline{Z}_{n}\leq M_{n},\lVert\hat{\eta}-\eta_{0}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{n},\mathrm{Sub}_{n}(\hat{G}_{n})>C_{1,\mathcal{H}}\epsilon_{n}}\right]\leq\frac{9}{n}.

The following corollary plugs in the rates (
OA1
) for
\Delta_{n},M_{n}
and
verifies that they satisfy
SM1.1
.

Corollary SM1.1
.

For
\beta\geq 0
, suppose
\Delta_{n},M_{n}
are of the form (
OA1
). Then there
exists a
C_{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
such that the following tail bound holds. Recall the average
Hellinger distance
\overline{h}
from
(
A.1
). Suppose
\hat{G}_{n}
satisfies
1
. Under

4
,
2
, and
3
, define

\varepsilon_{n}

as:

\varepsilon_{n}=n^{-\frac{p}{2p+1}}(\log n)^{\frac{2+\alpha}{2\alpha}+\beta}\overline{h}\left({f_{\hat{G}_{n},\bm{\cdot}},f_{G_{0},\bm{\cdot}}}\right)+n^{-\frac{2p}{2p+1}}(\log n)^{\frac{2+\alpha}{\alpha}+2\beta},

we have that,
\operatorname{P}\left[{A_{n},\mathrm{Sub}_{n}(\hat{G}_{n})>C_{\mathcal{H}}^{*}\varepsilon_{n}}\right]\leq\frac{9}{n}.

The constants in
\Delta_{n},M_{n}
affects the conclusion of the statement only through
affecting the constant
C_{\mathcal{H}}^{*}
.

Proof.

We first show that the specification of
\Delta_{n}
and
M_{n}
means that the requirements
of
SM1.1
are satisfied. Among the requirements of
SM1.1
:

(1)

is satisfied since the polynomial part of
\Delta_{n}
converges to zero slower
than
n^{-1/2}
, but converges to zero faster than any logarithmic rate.
M_{n}
is a
logarithmic rate.

(2)

is satisfied since
\alpha\leq 2
.

We also observe that by Jensen’s inequality,

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}},f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}})\leq\overline{h}(f_{\hat{G}_{n},\bm{\cdot}},f_{G_{0},\bm{\cdot}}),

and so we can replace the corresponding factor in
\epsilon_{n}
by
\overline{h}
.
Now, we plug the rates
\Delta_{n},M_{n}
into
\epsilon_{n}
. We find that the second term in

\epsilon_{n}

is dominated:

\Delta_{n}M_{n}^{2}e^{-C_{2,\mathcal{H}}M_{n}^{\alpha}}=\Delta_{n}M_{n}^{2}e^{-(C_{\mathcal{H}}+1)^{\alpha}(\log n)}\leq\Delta_{n}M_{n}^{2}n^{-1}\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\Delta_{n}^{2}M_{n}^{2}\log n

since
\log n>1
as
n>\sqrt{2\pi}e
by
1
. The fourth term is also
dominated by the third term. Thus, plugging in the rates for
the other terms, we find that
\epsilon_{n}\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\varepsilon_{n}.
Therefore,
SM1.1
follows from

Theorem

SM1.1
.
∎

SM1.1.
Derivative computations

It is sometimes useful to relate the derivatives of
\psi_{i}
to

\mathbf{E}_{G,\eta}
. We compute the following derivatives. Since they are all evaluated at

G,\eta
, we let
\hat{\nu}=\hat{\nu}_{i}(\eta)
and
\hat{z}=\hat{Z}_{i}(\eta)
as a shorthand.

(SM1.3)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial{\psi_{i}}}{\partial{m_{i}}}\bigg\rvert_{\eta,G}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{s_{i}}\frac{f^{\prime}_{G,\hat{\nu}}(\hat{z})}{f_{G,\hat{\nu}}(\hat{z})}

(SM1.4)

\displaystyle=\frac{s_{i}}{\sigma_{i}^{2}}\mathbf{E}_{G,\hat{\nu}}[Z-\tau\mid\hat{z}]

(SM1.5)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial{\psi_{i}}}{\partial{s_{i}}}\bigg\rvert_{{\eta,G}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}\hat{\nu}_{i}(\eta)f_{G,\hat{\nu}(\eta)}(\hat{Z}_{i}(\eta))}\underbrace{\int(\hat{Z}_{i}(\eta)-\tau)\tau\varphi\left({\frac{\hat{Z}_{i}(\eta)-\tau}{\hat{\nu}_{i}(\eta)}}\right)\frac{1}{\hat{\nu}_{i}(\eta)}\,G(d\tau)}_{Q_{i}(Z_{i},\eta,G)}

(SM1.6)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}\hat{\nu}}\mathbf{E}_{G,\hat{\nu}}[(Z-\tau)\tau\mid\hat{z}]

(SM1.7)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial{{}^{2}\psi_{i}}}{\partial{m_{i}^{2}}}\bigg\rvert_{\eta,G}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{s^{2}_{i}}\left[{\frac{f^{\prime\prime}_{G,\hat{\nu}}(\hat{z})}{f_{G,\hat{\nu}}(\hat{z})}-\left({\frac{f^{\prime}_{G,\hat{\nu}}(\hat{z})}{f_{G,\hat{\nu}}(\hat{z})}}\right)^{2}}\right]

(SM1.8)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{s_{i}^{2}}\left[{\frac{1}{\hat{\nu}^{4}}\mathbf{E}_{G,\hat{\nu}}[(\tau-Z)^{2}\mid\hat{z}]-\frac{1}{\hat{\nu}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\hat{\nu}^{4}}\left({\mathbf{E}_{G,\hat{\nu}}[(\tau-Z)\mid\hat{z}]}\right)^{2}}\right]

(SM1.9)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial{{}^{2}\psi_{i}}}{\partial{m_{i}\partial s_{i}}}\bigg\rvert_{\eta,G}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}f_{G,\hat{\nu}(\eta)}}\int\left\{{\left({\frac{\hat{Z}(\eta)-\tau}{\hat{\nu}(\eta)}}\right)^{2}-1}\right\}\tau\varphi\left({\frac{\hat{Z}(\eta)-\tau}{\hat{\nu}(\eta)}}\right)\frac{1}{\hat{\nu}(\eta)}G(d\tau)+\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\frac{Q_{i}(Z_{i},\eta,G)}{f_{G,\hat{\nu}(\eta)}}\cdot\frac{f^{\prime}_{G,\hat{\nu}(\eta)}}{f_{G,\hat{\nu}(\eta)}}

(SM1.10)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\mathbf{E}_{G,\hat{\nu}}\left[{\left\{{\left({\frac{Z-\tau}{\hat{\nu}}}\right)^{2}-1}\right\}\tau\mid\hat{z}}\right]+\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}\hat{\nu}(\eta)^{2}}\mathbf{E}_{G,\hat{\nu}}[(Z-\tau)\tau\mid\hat{z}]\mathbf{E}_{G,\hat{\nu}}[\tau-Z\mid\hat{z}]

(SM1.11)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial{{}^{2}\psi_{i}}}{\partial{s_{i}^{2}}}\bigg\rvert_{\eta,G}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}f_{G,\hat{\nu}(\eta)}}\int\left\{{\left({\frac{\hat{Z}(\eta)-\tau}{\hat{\nu}(\eta)}}\right)^{2}-1}\right\}\tau^{2}\varphi\left({\frac{\hat{Z}(\eta)-\tau}{\hat{\nu}(\eta)}}\right)\frac{1}{\hat{\nu}(\eta)}G(d\tau)+\frac{s_{i}^{2}}{\sigma^{4}}\left({\frac{Q_{i}(Z_{i},\eta,G)}{f_{G,\hat{\nu}(\eta)}}}\right)^{2}

(SM1.12)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\mathbf{E}_{G,\hat{\nu}}\left[{\tau^{2}\left\{{\left({\frac{Z-\tau}{\hat{\nu}}}\right)^{2}-1}\right\}}\right]+\frac{s_{i}^{2}}{\sigma^{4}}\mathbf{E}_{G,\hat{\nu}}[(Z-\tau)\tau\mid\hat{z}]^{2}.

It is also useful to note that

(SM1.13)

\displaystyle\frac{f^{\prime}_{G,\nu}(z)}{f_{G,\nu}(z)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}\mathbf{E}_{G,\nu}[(\tau-Z)\mid z]

(SM1.14)

\displaystyle\frac{f^{\prime\prime}_{G,\nu}(z)}{f_{G,\nu}(z)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\nu^{4}}\mathbf{E}_{G,\nu}[(\tau-Z)^{2}\mid z]-\frac{1}{\nu^{2}}.

SM1.2.
Proof of
Theorem

SM1.1

SM1.2.1.
Decomposition of
\mathrm{Sub}_{n}(\hat{G}_{n})

Observe that, by (
1
) in
1
,

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi_{i}(Z_{i},\hat{\eta},\hat{G}_{n})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi_{i}(Z_{i},\hat{\eta},G_{0})\geq\kappa_{n}

For random variables
a_{n},b_{n}
such that when
\overline{Z}_{n}\leq M_{n},\lVert\hat{\eta}-\eta_{0}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{n}
,

\displaystyle\bigg\lvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi_{i}(Z_{i},\hat{\eta},\hat{G}_{n})-\psi_{i}(Z_{i},\eta_{0},\hat{G}_{n})\bigg\rvert\leq a_{n}\hskip 9.24994pt\bigg\lvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi_{i}(Z_{i},\hat{\eta},G_{0})-\psi_{i}(Z_{i},\eta_{0},G_{0})\bigg\rvert\leq b_{n}

we have that on the event
\overline{Z}_{n}\leq M_{n},\lVert\hat{\eta}-\eta_{0}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{n}
,

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi_{i}(Z_{i},\eta_{0},\hat{G}_{n})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi_{i}(Z_{i},\eta_{0},G_{0})\geq-a_{n}-b_{n}-\kappa_{n}

and therefore
\mathrm{Sub}_{n}(\hat{G}_{n})\leq a_{n}+b_{n}+\kappa_{n}
.
Therefore, it suffices to show large deviation results for
a_{n}
and
b_{n}
, where
a_{n}

is chosen to be (
SM1.2.2
) and
b_{n}
is chosen to be (
SM1.2.3
):

\displaystyle\operatorname{P}\left[{\overline{Z}_{n}\leq M_{n},\lVert\hat{\eta}-\eta_{0}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{n},\mathrm{Sub}_{n}(\hat{G}_{n})\gtrsim_{\mathcal{H}}\epsilon_{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{P}\left[{\overline{Z}_{n}\leq M_{n},\lVert\hat{\eta}-\eta_{0}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{n},a_{n}+b_{n}+\kappa_{n}\gtrsim_{\mathcal{H}}\epsilon_{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{P}\left[{a_{n}+b_{n}+\kappa_{n}\gtrsim_{\mathcal{H}}\epsilon_{n}}\right].

SM1.2.2.
Taylor expansion of
\psi_{i}(Z_{i},\hat{\eta},\hat{G}_{n})-\psi_{i}(Z_{i},\eta_{0},\hat{G}_{n})

Define

\Delta_{mi}=\hat{m}_{i}-m_{0i}
,
\Delta_{si}=\hat{s}_{i}-s_{0i}
, and
\Delta_{i}=[\Delta_{mi},\Delta_{si}]^{\prime}
. Recall
\lVert\hat{\eta}-\eta_{0}\rVert_{\infty}=\max(\lVert s-s_{0}\rVert_{\infty},\lVert m-m_{0}\rVert_{\infty})
as in (
A.1
).
Since
\psi_{i}(Z_{i},\eta,G)
is smooth in
(m_{i},s_{i})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, we can take
a second-order Taylor expansion:

(SM1.15)

\displaystyle\psi_{i}\left({Z_{i},\hat{\eta},\hat{G}_{n}}\right)-\psi_{i}\left({Z_{i},\eta_{0},\hat{G}_{n}}\right)=\frac{\partial{\psi_{i}}}{\partial{m_{i}}}\bigg\rvert_{\eta_{0},\hat{G}_{n}}\Delta_{mi}+\frac{\partial{\psi_{i}}}{\partial{s_{i}}}\bigg\rvert_{\eta_{0},\hat{G}_{n}}\Delta_{si}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{i}^{\prime}H_{i}(\tilde{\eta}_{i},\hat{G}_{n})\Delta_{i}}_{R_{1i}}

where
H_{i}(\tilde{\eta}_{i},\hat{G}_{n})
is the Hessian matrix
\frac{\partial{{}^{2}\psi_{i}}}{\partial{\eta_{i}\partial\eta_{i}^{\prime}}}
evaluated at some intermediate value
\tilde{\eta}_{i}
lying on the line segment
between
\hat{\eta}_{i}
and
\eta_{0i}
.

We further decompose the first-order terms into an empirical process term and a
mean-component term. By
Lemma

OA1.1
, (
SM1.4
), and (
SM1.5
), for the
choice
\rho_{n}
in (
OA1
) we have that the denominators to the first
derivatives can be truncated at
\rho_{n}
, as
f_{i,\hat{G}_{n}}\geq\rho_{n}/\nu_{i}
so that
the truncation does not bind:

(SM1.16)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial{\psi_{i}}}{\partial{m_{i}}}\bigg\rvert_{\eta_{0},\hat{G}_{n}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{s_{i}}\frac{f^{\prime}_{i,\hat{G}_{n}}}{f_{i,\hat{G}_{n}}\vee\frac{\rho_{n}}{\nu_{i}}}\equiv D_{m,i}(Z_{i},\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})

(SM1.17)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial{\psi_{i}}}{\partial{s_{i}}}\bigg\rvert_{\eta_{0},\hat{G}_{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{s_{i}}{\sigma_{i}^{2}}\frac{Q_{i}(Z_{i},\eta_{0},\hat{G}_{n})}{f_{i,\hat{G}_{n}}\vee\frac{\rho_{n}}{\nu_{i}}}\equiv D_{s,i}(Z_{i},\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n}).

where we recall
Q_{i}
from (
SM1.5
).

Let

\overline{D}_{k,i}(\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}D_{k,i}(z,\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})\,f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}}(z)dz\hskip 9.24994pt\text{ for $k\in\left\{{m,s}\right\}$}

be the population mean of
D_{k,i}
.
Then, for
k\in\left\{{m,s}\right\}
, we can decompose

\frac{\partial{\psi_{i}}}{\partial{k_{i}}}\bigg\rvert_{\eta_{0},\hat{G}_{n}}\Delta_{ki}=\left[{D_{k,i}(Z_{i},\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})-\overline{D}_{k,i}(\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})}\right]\Delta_{ki}+\overline{D}_{k,i}(\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})\Delta_{ki}

Hence, we can decompose the first-order terms in (
SM1.15
):

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial{\psi_{i}}}{\partial{k_{i}}}\bigg\rvert_{\eta_{0},\hat{G}_{n}}\Delta_{ki}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\overline{D}_{k,i}(\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})\Delta_{ki}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[{D_{k,i}(Z_{i},\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})-\overline{D}_{k,i}(\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})}\right]\Delta_{ki}

\displaystyle\equiv U_{1k}+U_{2k}.

Let the second order term in (
SM1.15
) be denoted as
R_{1}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}R_{1i}
. We let

a_{n}=|R_{1}|+\sum_{k\in\left\{{m,s}\right\}}|U_{1k}|+|U_{2k}|.

SM1.2.3.
Taylor expansion of
\psi_{i}(Z_{i},\hat{\eta},G_{0})-\psi_{i}(Z_{i},\eta_{0},G_{0})

Like (
SM1.15
), we similarly decompose

(SM1.20)

\displaystyle\psi_{i}(Z_{i},\hat{\eta},G_{0})-\psi_{i}(Z_{i},\eta_{0},G_{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial{\psi_{i}}}{\partial{m_{i}}}\bigg\rvert_{\eta_{0},G_{0}}\Delta_{mi}+\frac{\partial{\psi_{i}}}{\partial{s_{i}}}\bigg\rvert_{\eta_{0},G_{0}}\Delta_{si}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{i}^{\prime}H_{i}(\tilde{\eta}_{i},G_{0})\Delta_{i}}_{R_{2i}}

(SM1.21)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in\left\{{m,s}\right\}}D_{k,i}(Z_{i},G_{0},\eta_{0},0)\Delta_{ki}+R_{2i}\equiv U_{3mi}+U_{3si}+R_{2i}.

Let
U_{3k}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}U_{3ki}
for
k\in\left\{{m,s}\right\}
and let
R_{2}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}R_{2i}
. We let

b_{n}=|R_{2}|+\sum_{k\in\left\{{m,s}\right\}}|U_{3k}|+|U_{3k}|.

SM1.2.4.
Bounding each term individually

By our decomposition, we can write

a_{n}+b_{n}+\kappa_{n}\leq\kappa_{n}+|R_{1}|+|R_{2}|+\sum_{k\in\left\{{m,s}\right\}}|U_{1k}|+|U_{2k}|+|U_{3k}|.

To summarize, we have that, for
k=m,s,

(SM1.23)

\displaystyle U_{1k}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\overline{D}_{k,i}(\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})\Delta_{ki}

(SM1.24)

\displaystyle U_{2k}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[{D_{k,i}(Z_{i},\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})-\overline{D}_{k,i}(\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})}\right]\Delta_{ki}

(SM1.25)

\displaystyle U_{3k}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{k,i}(Z_{i},G_{0},\eta_{0},0)\Delta_{ki}

(SM1.26)

\displaystyle R_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Delta_{i}^{\prime}H_{i}(\tilde{\eta}_{i},\hat{G}_{n})\Delta_{i}

(SM1.27)

\displaystyle R_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Delta_{i}^{\prime}H_{i}(\tilde{\eta}_{i},G_{0})\Delta_{i}

The ensuing subsections bound each term individually. Here we give an overview of the
main ideas:

(1)

We bound
\mathbbm{1}(A_{n})|U_{1m}|
in
Lemma

SM1.1
by observing that
|\overline{D}_{mi}(\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})|
is small when
\hat{G}_{n}
is close to
G_{0}
, since

\overline{D}_{mi}(G_{0},\eta_{0},0)=0
. To do so, we need to control the differences

\displaystyle\overline{D}_{mi}(\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})-\overline{D}_{mi}(G_{0},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})\text{ and }\overline{D}_{mi}(G_{0},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})-\underbrace{\overline{D}_{mi}(G_{0},\eta_{0},0)}_{=0}=\overline{D}_{mi}(G_{0},\eta_{0},\rho_{n}).

Controlling the first difference features the Hellinger distance, while controlling
the second relies on the fact that
\operatorname{P}_{X\sim f(X)}(f(X)\leq\rho)
cannot be too
large, by a Chebyshev’s inequality argument in
Lemma

SM1.9
. Similarly, we bound
\mathbbm{1}(A_{n})|U_{1s}|
in
Lemma

SM1.2
.

(2)

The empirical process terms
U_{2m},U_{2s}
are bounded with statements of the form

\operatorname{P}(A_{n},|U_{2k}|>r_{n})\leq 2/n.

To do so, we upper bound
\mathbbm{1}(A_{n})U_{2k}\leq\overline{U}_{2k}
. The upper bound is
obtained by projecting
\hat{G}_{n}
onto a
\omega
-net of
\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R})
in terms
of some pseudo-metric
d_{k,\infty,M_{n}}
induced by
\overline{D}_{k,i}
. The upper bound

\overline{U}_{2k}
then takes the form (up to some other terms), for
\eta\in S
over a
Hölder space and
G_{1},\ldots,G_{N}
a
\omega
-net over
\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R})
in
d_{k,\infty,M_{n}}
,

\omega\Delta_{n}+\max_{j\in[N]}\sup_{\eta\in S}\bigg\lvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}(D_{ki}(G_{j},\eta,\rho_{n})-\overline{D}_{ki}(G_{j},\eta,\rho_{n}))(\eta_{i}-\eta_{0i})\bigg\rvert\hskip 9.24994ptN\leq N(\omega,\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}),d_{k,\infty,M_{n}}).

Large deviation of
\overline{U}_{2k}
is further controlled by applying Dudley’s tail bound

(
Vershynin 2018h
)
, since the entropy integral over
S
is well-behaved. The
covering number
N
is controlled via
SM1.1
and

SM1.2
, which are minor extensions to Lemma 4 and Theorem 7 in

Jiang 2020h
. The covering number is of a manageable size since the
induced distributions
f_{G,\nu_{i}}
are very smooth.

(3)

Since
\overline{D}_{k,i}(G_{0},\eta_{0},0)=0
.
U_{3m},U_{3s}
are effectively also
empirical process terms, without the additional randomness in
\hat{G}_{n}
. Thus the
projection-to-
\omega
-net argument above is unnecessary for
U_{3m},U_{3s}
, whereas the
bounding follows from the same Dudley’s chaining argument.
Lemma

SM1.4
bounds

U_{3k}
.

(4)

For the second derivative terms
R_{1},R_{2}
, we observe that the second derivatives
take the form of functions of posterior moments under either
\hat{G}_{n}
or
G_{0}
. The
posterior moments under prior
\hat{G}_{n}
is bounded within constant factors of
M_{n}^{q}

since the support of
G_{n}
is restricted. The posterior moments under prior
G_{0}
is
bounded by
|Z_{i}|^{q}\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}M_{n}^{q}
as we show in
Lemma

SM1.14
, thanks to
the simultaneous moment control for
G_{0}
. These second derivatives are bounded in
Lemmas

SM1.5
and
SM1.6
.

(1) and (4) above bounds
U_{1k},R_{1},R_{2}
under
A_{n}
. (2) and (3) bounds
U_{2k},U_{3k}
probabilistically by bounding
\operatorname{P}[A_{n},U_{jk}>t]
. By a union bound in

Lemma

SM1.13
, we can simply add the rates.

Doing so, we find that the first term in
\epsilon_{n}

(
SM1.1
) comes from
U_{1s}
, which dominates
U_{1m}
. The second term
comes from
U_{2k}
. The third term comes from
R_{1}
, which
dominates
R_{2}
; this term also dominates a term in the bound for
U_{2k}
. The fourth
term comes from
U_{3s}
. The leading terms in
\epsilon_{n}

dominate
\kappa_{n}
, recalling
1
. This completes the proof.

SM1.3.
Bounding
U_{1m}

Lemma SM1.1
.

Under
1
,
2
,
4
, and
3
, assume additionally that

\lVert\hat{\eta}-\eta_{0}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{n},\overline{Z}_{n}\leq M_{n}
. Assume that the rates
\Delta_{n},M_{n}
satisfy

SM1.1
.
Then

|U_{1m}|\equiv\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\overline{D}_{mi}(\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})\Delta_{mi}\right|\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\Delta_{n}\left[{\frac{\log n}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}},f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{I}})+\frac{M_{n}^{1/3}}{n}}\right].

Proof.

Note that

\displaystyle|\overline{D}_{m,i}(\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})|=|\eqref{eq:D_bar_def}|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s_{0\ell}}\Bigg\lvert\int\frac{f^{\prime}_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)}{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)\vee\frac{\rho_{n}}{\nu_{i}}}\,f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}}(z)dz\Bigg\rvert

\displaystyle=\Bigg\lvert\int\frac{f^{\prime}_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)}{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)\vee\frac{\rho_{n}}{\nu_{i}}}\,[f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}}(z)-f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)+f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)]dz\Bigg\rvert

(SM1.29)

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg\lvert\int\frac{f^{\prime}_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)}{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)\vee\frac{\rho_{n}}{\nu_{i}}}\,[f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}}(z)-f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)]\,dz\Bigg\rvert

(SM1.30)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt+\Bigg\lvert\int\frac{f^{\prime}_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)}{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)\vee\frac{\rho_{n}}{\nu_{i}}}f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)dz\Bigg\rvert.

By the bounds for (
SM1.29
) and (
SM1.30
) below, we have that

|U_{1m}|\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\Delta_{n}\left[{\frac{\sqrt{\log n}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}},f_{\hat{G}_{n},\hat{\nu}_{i}})+\frac{M_{n}^{1/3}}{n}}\right]

by
SM1.1
.
∎

SM1.3.1.
Bounding (
SM1.29
)

Consider the first term (
SM1.29
):

\displaystyle[\eqref{eq:dmi_first}]^{2}

\displaystyle=\left[{\int\frac{f^{\prime}_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)}{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)\vee\frac{\rho_{n}}{\nu_{i}}}\left({\sqrt{f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}}(z)}-\sqrt{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)}}\right)\left({\sqrt{f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}}(z)}+\sqrt{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)}}\right)\,dz}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\int\left({\sqrt{f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}}(z)}-\sqrt{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)}}\right)^{2}\,dz}_{2h^{2}(f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}},f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}})}

(Cauchy–Schwarz)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\times\int\left({\frac{f^{\prime}_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)}{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)\vee\frac{\rho_{n}}{\nu_{i}}}}\right)^{2}\left({\sqrt{f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}}(z)}+\sqrt{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)}}\right)^{2}\,dz

(SM1.31)

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2}(f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}},f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}})\int\left({\frac{f^{\prime}_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)}{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)\vee\frac{\rho_{n}}{\nu_{i}}}}\right)^{2}(f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}}(z)+f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z))\,dz

where the last step uses
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
. By
Lemmas

SM1.8
and
OA1.1
,

\left({\frac{f^{\prime}_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)}{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)\vee\frac{\rho_{n}}{\nu_{i}}}}\right)^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\nu_{i}}\log(1/\rho_{n})\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\log n.

Hence,
\eqref{eq:dmi_first}\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}h(f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}},f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}})\sqrt{\log n}.

SM1.3.2.
Bounding (
SM1.30
)

The second term (
SM1.30
) is

(
SM1.30
)

\displaystyle=\Bigg\lvert\int\frac{f^{\prime}_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)}{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)}\left({\frac{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)}{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)\vee\frac{\rho_{n}}{\nu_{i}}}-1}\right)f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)\,dz\Bigg\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\int\Bigg\lvert\frac{f^{\prime}_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)}{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)}\Bigg\rvert\mathbbm{1}\left({f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)\leq\rho_{n}/\nu_{i}}\right)f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)\,dz

(Cauchy–Schwarz and (
SM1.13
))

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\left({\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}}\left[{\left({\mathbf{E}_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}\left[{\frac{(\tau-Z)}{\nu_{i}^{2}}\mid Z}\right]}\right)^{2}}\right]}\right)^{1/2}}_{\leq\mathbb{E}_{\tau\sim\hat{G}_{n},Z\sim\mathcal{N}(\tau,\nu_{i})}[(\tau-Z)^{2}/\nu_{i}^{4}]^{1/2}=\nu_{i}^{-1}}\cdot\sqrt{\operatorname{P}_{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}}[f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(Z)\leq\rho_{n}/\nu_{i}]}.

By Jensen’s inequality and law of iterated expectations, the first term is bounded by

\frac{1}{\nu_{i}}
. By
Lemma

SM1.9
, the second term is bounded by
\rho_{n}^{1/3}\operatorname{Var}_{Z\sim f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}}(Z)^{1/6}
. Now,
\operatorname{Var}_{Z\sim f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}}(Z)\leq\nu_{i}^{2}+\mu_{2}^{2}(\hat{G}_{n})\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}M_{n}^{2}
.
Hence, by
Lemma

OA1.1
,

\eqref{eq:dmi_second}\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}M_{n}^{1/3}\rho_{n}^{1/3}\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}M_{n}^{1/3}n^{-1}.

SM1.4.
Bounding
U_{1s}

Lemma SM1.2
.

Under
1
,
2
,
4
,
3
, and
SM1.1
, if
\lVert\hat{\eta}-\eta_{0}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{n}
,

\overline{Z}_{n}\leq M_{n}
, then

|U_{1s}|\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\Delta_{n}\left[{\frac{M_{n}\sqrt{\log n}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}},f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}})+\frac{M_{n}^{4/3}}{n}}\right].

Proof.

Similar to our computation with
\overline{D}_{m,i}
, we
decompose

(SM1.33)

\displaystyle|\overline{D}_{s,i}(\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\bigg\lvert\int\frac{Q_{i}(z,\eta_{0},\hat{G}_{n})}{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)\vee(\rho_{n}/\nu_{i})}(f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}}(z)-f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z))\,dz\bigg\rvert

(SM1.34)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\bigg\lvert\int\frac{Q_{i}(z,\eta_{0},\hat{G}_{n})}{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)\vee(\rho_{n}/\nu_{i})}f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)\,dz\bigg\rvert,

where we recall
Q_{i}
from
(
SM1.5
).
We conclude the proof by plugging in our subsequent calculations.
∎

SM1.4.1.
Bounding (
SM1.33
)

The first term (
SM1.33
) is bounded by

\displaystyle[\eqref{eq:dsi_first}]^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{2}(f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}},f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}})\int\left({\frac{Q_{i}(z,\eta_{0},\hat{G}_{n})}{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)\vee(\rho_{n}/\nu_{i})}}\right)^{2}\left[{f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}}(z)+f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)}\right]\,dz,

similar to the computation in (
SM1.31
).

By
Lemmas

SM1.10
and
OA1.1
,

\left({\frac{Q_{i}(z,\eta_{0},\hat{G}_{n})}{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)\vee(\rho_{n}/\nu_{i})}}\right)^{2}\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}M_{n}^{2}\log n\implies\int\left({\frac{Q(z,\nu_{i})}{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\hat{\nu}_{i}}(z)\vee(\rho_{n}/\nu_{i})}}\right)^{2}\left[{f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}}(z)+f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)}\right]\,dz\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}M_{n}^{2}\log n.

Hence,

\eqref{eq:dsi_first}\lesssim_{\sigma_{\ell},\sigma_{u},s_{0\ell},s_{0u}}M_{n}h(f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}},f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}})\sqrt{\log n}.

SM1.4.2.
Bounding (
SM1.34
)

Observe that

\eqref{eq:dsi_third}=\Bigg\lvert\int\underbrace{\frac{Q_{i}(z,\eta_{0},\hat{G}_{n})}{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)}}_{\mathbf{E}_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}[(\hat{Z}-\tau)\tau\mid\hat{Z}=z]}\underbrace{\left({\frac{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)}{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)\vee(\rho_{n}/\nu_{i})}-1}\right)}_{|\cdot|\leq\mathbbm{1}(\nu_{i}f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}\leq\rho_{n})}f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)\,dz\Bigg\rvert.

Similar to our argument for (
SM1.30
), by Cauchy–Schwarz,

(
SM1.34
)

\displaystyle\leq\left({\mathbb{E}_{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)}\left[{(\mathbf{E}_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}\left[{(Z-\tau)\tau\mid Z}\right])^{2}}\right]}\right)^{1/2}\sqrt{\operatorname{P}_{f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)}(f_{\hat{G}_{n},\nu_{i}}(z)\leq\rho_{n}/\nu_{i})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}M_{n}\cdot\rho_{n}^{1/3}M_{n}^{1/3}\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\frac{M_{n}^{4/3}}{n}.

SM1.5.
Bounding
U_{2m},U_{2s}

Lemma SM1.3
.

Under
1
,
2
,
4
,
3
, and
SM1.1
,
for
k\in\left\{{m,s}\right\}
,

\operatorname{P}\left[{\lVert\hat{\eta}-\eta_{0}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{n},\overline{Z}_{n}\leq M_{n},|U_{2k}|\gtrsim_{\mathcal{H}}r_{n}}\right]\leq\frac{2}{n}

for
r_{n}=\Delta_{n}e^{-C_{\mathcal{H}}M_{n}^{\alpha}}\log n+\frac{M_{n}^{3/2}(\log n)^{5/4}}{\sqrt{n}}\Delta_{n}+\frac{M_{n}\sqrt{\log n}}{\sqrt{n}}\Delta_{n}^{1-\frac{1}{2p}}.

Proof.

Let
k\in\left\{{m,s}\right\}
.
We first show that if
\lVert\hat{\eta}-\eta_{0}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{n}
and
\overline{Z}_{n}\leq M_{n}
, then for some
\overline{U}_{2k}
to be chosen,
|U_{2k}|\leq\overline{U}_{2k}.

We choose
\overline{U}_{2k}
in (
SM1.5
) such that
\operatorname{P}[\overline{U}_{2k}>t]
is small. Then

\operatorname{P}\left[{\lVert\hat{\eta}-\eta_{0}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{n},\overline{Z}_{n}\leq M_{n},|U_{2k}|>t}\right]\leq\operatorname{P}[\overline{U}_{2k}>t].

Thus the bound for
\overline{U}_{2k}
would suffice.

Let

\displaystyle D_{k,i,M_{n}}(Z_{i},\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})

\displaystyle=D_{k,i}(Z_{i},\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})\mathbbm{1}(|Z_{i}|\leq M_{n})

\displaystyle\overline{D}_{k,i,M_{n}}(\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})

\displaystyle=\int D_{k,i}(z,\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})\mathbbm{1}(|z|\leq M_{n})f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}}(z)\,dz.

They are truncated versions of
D_{k,i}(z,\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})
.

Observe that on
\overline{Z}_{n}\leq M_{n}
,
D_{k,i,M_{n}}(Z_{i},\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})=D_{k,i}(Z_{i},\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})
for all
i
. Thus, on
\overline{Z}_{n}\leq M_{n}
, we may
decompose

(SM1.36)

\displaystyle|U_{2k}|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\{{D_{k,i,M_{n}}(Z_{i},\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})-\overline{D}_{k,i,M_{n}}(\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})}\right\}\Delta_{ki}\right|

(SM1.37)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\{{\overline{D}_{k,i}(Z_{i},\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})-\overline{D}_{k,i,M_{n}}(\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})}\right\}\Delta_{ki}\right|.

By
Lemmas

SM1.10
and
SM1.8
, uniformly over all
G
,

|D_{k,i}(z,G,\eta_{0},\rho_{n})|\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}|z|\sqrt{\log n}+\log n.

Thus,

\eqref{eq:Dk_tail}\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\Delta_{n}\bigg({\sqrt{\log n}\max_{i\in[n]}\underbrace{\int_{|z|>M_{n}}|z|f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}}(z)\,dz}_{\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}^{2}]\operatorname{P}\left({|Z_{i}|>M_{n}}\right)}\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\operatorname{P}\left({|Z_{i}|>M_{n}}\right)^{1/2}}+\log n\max_{i\in[n]}\operatorname{P}_{G_{0},\nu_{i}}(|Z_{i}|>M_{n})}\bigg)

By
Lemma

SM1.12
,
\operatorname{P}_{G_{0},\nu_{i}}(|Z_{i}|>M_{n})\leq\exp\left({-C_{\alpha,A_{0},\nu_{u}}M_{n}^{\alpha}}\right).
Hence
\eqref{eq:Dk_tail}\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\Delta_{n}e^{-C_{\mathcal{H}}M_{n}^{\alpha}}\log n.

Returning to (
SM1.36
), let
G_{1},\ldots,G_{N}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R})
be
a
\omega
-net of
\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R})
under the pseudometric

d_{k,\infty,M_{n}}(G_{1},G_{2})=\max_{i\in[n]}\sup_{|z|\leq M_{n}}\lvert D_{k,i}(z,G_{1},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})-D_{m,i}(z,G_{2},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})\rvert.

Thus, we can take
N=N(\omega,\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}),d_{k,\infty,M_{n}})
. By construction,
there exists a
G_{j^{*}}
for
j^{*}\in[N]
such that on
\overline{Z}_{n}\leq M_{n}
,

\displaystyle\lvert D_{k,i,M_{n}}(Z_{i},\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})-D_{k,i,M_{n}}(Z_{i},G_{j^{*}},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\omega

\displaystyle\implies\lvert\overline{D}_{k,i,M_{n}}(\hat{G}_{n},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})-\overline{D}_{k,i,M_{n}}(G_{j^{*}},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\omega.

The second line in the above display holds since the integrand is bounded by
\omega
.
Hence, projecting
\hat{G}_{n}
to the
\omega
-net, we have that

\eqref{eq:pre_project_emp_proc}\leq 2\omega\Delta_{n}+\max_{j\in[N]}\bigg\lvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\{{D_{k,i,M_{n}}(Z_{i},G_{j},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})-\overline{D}_{k,i,M_{n}}(G_{j},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})}\right\}\Delta_{ki}\bigg\rvert

Define

\displaystyle v_{i,j}(\eta)

\displaystyle\equiv\left\{{D_{k,i,M_{n}}(Z_{i},G_{j},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})-\overline{D}_{k,i,M_{n}}(G_{j},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})}\right\}\Delta_{ki}(\eta)\hskip 9.24994pt\Delta_{ki}(\eta)\equiv k_{i}(\sigma_{i})-k_{0}(\sigma_{i})\hskip 9.24994ptk\in\left\{{m,s}\right\}

\displaystyle V_{n,j}(\eta)

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i,j}(\eta).

We have that

\eqref{eq:pre_project_emp_proc}\lesssim\omega\Delta_{n}+\max_{j\in[N]}\sup_{\eta\in S}|V_{n,j}(\eta)|

where

S=\left\{{(m,s):\lVert m-m_{0}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{n},\lVert s-s_{0}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{n},(m,s)\in\mathcal{V}}\right\}

for
\mathcal{V}
in

4
.
As a result, for some
\omega
to be chosen, let us take

\overline{U}_{2k}=C_{\mathcal{H}}\left\{{\Delta_{n}(\log n)e^{-C_{\mathcal{H}}M_{n}^{\alpha}}+\omega\Delta_{n}+\max_{j\in[N]}\sup_{\eta\in S}|V_{n,j}(\eta)|}\right\}

and analyze its tail behavior.

First, let us bound the empirical process
\max_{j\in[N]}\sup_{\eta\in S}|V_{n,j}(\eta)|
. Note that for a fixed
\eta,\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\in S
, we have that, since
\eqref{eq:Dki_bound_}\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}M_{n}\sqrt{\log n}
on
|z|\leq M_{n}
,

\displaystyle\left|V_{n,j}(\eta)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\frac{M_{n}\sqrt{\log n}}{\sqrt{n}}\Delta_{n}

(
v_{i,j}
are independent across
i
and bounded)

\displaystyle\lVert V_{n,j}(\eta_{1})-V_{n,j}(\eta_{2})\rVert_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\frac{M_{n}\sqrt{\log n}}{\sqrt{n}}\lVert\eta_{1}-\eta_{2}\rVert_{\infty}.

Since
\eta\mapsto V_{n,j}(\eta)
is a process with subgaussian increments under
\lVert\eta\rVert_{\infty}
(see (8.1)
in
Vershynin 2018h
), by Theorem 8.1.6 in

Vershynin 2018h
, for all
u>0
,

\sup_{\eta\in S}|V_{n,j}(\eta)|\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\frac{M_{n}\sqrt{\log n}}{\sqrt{n}}\left[{(1+u)\Delta_{n}+\int_{0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\log N(\epsilon,S,\lVert\cdot\rVert_{\infty})}\,d\epsilon}\right],

holds with probability at least
1-2e^{-u^{2}}
.

Since
\sqrt{\log N(\epsilon,S,\lVert\cdot\rVert_{\infty})}\lesssim\sqrt{\log N(\epsilon/2,\mathcal{V},\lVert\cdot\rVert_{\infty})}\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\sqrt{\log C_{\mathcal{H}}+(1/\epsilon)^{-1/p}}
by

4
and Exercise 4.2.10 in
Vershynin 2018h
,

\int_{0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\log N(\epsilon,S,\lVert\cdot\rVert_{\infty})}\,d\epsilon=\int_{0}^{2\Delta_{n}}\sqrt{\log N(\epsilon,S,\lVert\cdot\rVert_{\infty})}\,d\epsilon\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\Delta_{n}^{1-\frac{1}{2p}}.

Note that by a union bound,

\displaystyle\operatorname{P}\left\{{\max_{j\in[N]}\sup_{\eta\in S}|V_{n,j}(\eta)|\gtrsim_{\mathcal{H}}\frac{M_{n}\sqrt{\log n}}{\sqrt{n}}\left[{(1+u)\Delta_{n}+\Delta_{n}^{1-\frac{1}{2p}}}\right]}\right\}\leq 2Ne^{-u^{2}}

Choose
u=\sqrt{\log N}+\sqrt{\log n}\geq\sqrt{\log N+\log n}
such that the right
hand side is bounded by
2/n
.

Next, choose
\omega=M_{n}\frac{\sqrt{\log(1/\rho_{n})}}{\rho_{n}}\frac{\rho_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}\log\left({\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\rho_{n}}}\right)\geq\frac{\sqrt{\log(1/\rho_{n})}}{\rho_{n}}\frac{\rho_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}\log\left({\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\rho_{n}}}\right)
. By
SM1.2
,

\displaystyle\log N(\omega,\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}),d_{m,\infty,M_{n}})

\displaystyle\leq\log N\left({\frac{\sqrt{\log(1/\rho_{n})}}{\rho_{n}}\frac{\rho_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}\log\left({\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\rho_{n}}}\right),\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}),d_{m,\infty,M_{n}}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}(\log n)^{2}\max\left({1,\frac{M_{n}}{\sqrt{\log(n)}}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}(\log n)^{3/2}M_{n}

\displaystyle\log N(\omega,\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}),d_{s,\infty,M_{n}})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}(\log n)^{2}\max\left({1,\frac{M_{n}}{\sqrt{\log(n)}}}\right)

(
\delta=\rho_{n}/\sqrt{n}
)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}(\log n)^{3/2}M_{n}

Note that this choice is such that
\omega\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}(\log n)^{3/2}M_{n}
and

(1+u)\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}(\log n)^{3/4}M_{n}^{1/2}+\sqrt{\log n}\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}(\log n)^{3/4}M_{n}^{1/2}.

Returning to (
SM1.5
), since
V_{n,j}(\eta)
is the only random expression in
(
SM1.5
), this shows that

\operatorname{P}\left\{{\overline{U}_{2k}\gtrsim_{\mathcal{H}}\Delta_{n}e^{-C_{\mathcal{H}}M_{n}^{\alpha}}\log n+\frac{M_{n}^{3/2}(\log n)^{5/4}}{\sqrt{n}}\Delta_{n}+\frac{M_{n}\sqrt{\log n}}{\sqrt{n}}\Delta_{n}^{1-\frac{1}{2p}}}\right\}\leq\frac{2}{n}.

Here, note that the term
\omega\Delta_{n}
is dominated by
\frac{M_{n}^{3/2}(\log n)^{5/4}}{\sqrt{n}}\Delta_{n}
since
M_{n}\gtrsim_{\mathcal{H}}\sqrt{\log n}
. This concludes the
proof. ∎

SM1.6.
Bounding
U_{3m},U_{3s}

Lemma SM1.4
.

Under
2
,
4
,
3
, and
SM1.1
,
for
k\in\left\{{m,s}\right\}
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{P}\left[{\lVert\hat{\eta}-\eta_{0}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{n},\overline{Z}_{n}\leq M_{n},|U_{3k}|\gtrsim_{\mathcal{H}}\Delta_{n}\left\{{e^{-C_{\mathcal{H}}M_{n}^{\alpha}}+\frac{M_{n}^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\left({\Delta_{n}^{-1/(2p)}+\sqrt{\log n}}\right)}\right\}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{n}.

Proof.

The proof structure follows that of
Lemma

SM1.3
. Recall that

\displaystyle U_{3k}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{k,i}(Z_{i},G_{0},\eta_{0},0)\Delta_{ki}.

(on the event
\overline{Z}_{n}\leq M_{n}
)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\{{D_{k,i,M_{n}}(Z_{i},G_{0},\eta_{0},0)-\overline{D}_{k,i,M_{n}}(Z_{i},G_{0},\eta_{0},0)}\right\}\Delta_{ki}}_{V_{n}(\eta)}+\overline{D}_{k,i,M_{n}}(G_{0},\eta_{0},0)\Delta_{ki}.

Now, observe that, by Cauchy–Schwarz,

\displaystyle\left|\overline{D}_{k,i,M_{n}}(G_{0},\eta_{0},0)\right|\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\int_{|z|\leq M_{n}}T_{k}(z,\eta_{0},G_{0})f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}}(z)\,dz\leq\operatorname{P}(Z_{i}>M_{n})^{1/2}\left({\mathbb{E}\left[{T_{k}^{2}(Z_{i},\eta_{0},G_{0})}\right]}\right)^{1/2}

where
T_{m}=\frac{|f^{\prime}_{G_{0},\nu_{i}}(z)|}{f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}}(z_{i})}
and
T_{s}=\frac{|Q_{i}(z),\eta_{0},G_{0})|}{f_{G_{0},\nu_{i}}(z_{i})}
. Note that since both
T_{k}
take the form of
|\mathbb{E}_{G_{0}}[f(Z,\tau)\mid Z]|
, we can use Jensen’s inequality to bound
\mathbb{E}[T_{k}^{2}]\leq\mathbb{E}[f^{2}(Z_{i},\tau)]\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}1
.
Hence,

\left|\overline{D}_{k,i,M_{n}}(G_{0},\eta_{0},0)\right|\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}e^{-C_{\mathcal{H}}M_{n}^{\alpha}}.

We likewise analyze
V_{n}(\eta)
. Note that

\lvert V_{n}(\eta_{1})-V_{n}(\eta_{2})\rvert\leq\lVert\eta_{1}-\eta_{2}\rVert_{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|D_{k,i,M_{n}}(Z_{i},G_{0},\eta_{0},0)-\overline{D}_{k,i,M_{n}}(Z_{i},G_{0},\eta_{0},0)\right|.

By
Lemma

SM1.14
, since
D_{k,i,M_{n}}
is a posterior moment under
G_{0}

truncated to

|z|\leq M_{n}
,

\left|D_{k,i,M_{n}}(Z_{i},G_{0},\eta_{0},0)-\overline{D}_{k,i,M_{n}}(Z_{i},G_{0},\eta_{0},0)\right|\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}M_{n}^{2}.

As a result,

\bigg\lVert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left|D_{k,i,M_{n}}(Z_{i},G_{0},\eta_{0},0)-\overline{D}_{k,i,M_{n}}(Z_{i},G_{0},\eta_{0},0)\right|\bigg\rVert_{\psi_{2}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\frac{M_{n}^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}

and thus
\lVert V_{n}(\eta_{1})-V_{n}(\eta_{2})\rVert_{\psi_{2}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\frac{M_{n}^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\lVert\eta_{1}-\eta_{2}\rVert_{\infty}
has subgaussian increments. For a fixed
\eta
,
|V_{n}(\eta)|\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\Delta_{n}M_{n}^{2}/\sqrt{n}
.

By the same chaining argument as in the proof of
Lemma

SM1.3
, recalling
S
in (
SM1.5
),

\sup_{\eta\in S}|V_{n}(\eta)|\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\frac{M_{n}^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\left[{\sqrt{\log n}\Delta_{n}+\Delta_{n}^{1-\frac{1}{2p}}}\right]

with probability at least
1-2/n
. Here we choose
u=\sqrt{\log n}
since we do not have
to project
G_{0}
to some covering. Thus, we can let

\overline{U}_{3k}=C_{\mathcal{H}}\left({\sup_{\eta\in S}|V_{n}(\eta)|+\Delta_{n}e^{-C_{\mathcal{H}}M_{n}^{\alpha}}}\right).

Bounding
\overline{U}_{3k}
using the bound for
\sup_{\eta\in S}|V_{n}(\eta)|
concludes the
proof. ∎

SM1.7.
Bounding
R_{1},R_{2}

Lemma SM1.5
.

Recall
R_{1i}
from (
SM1.15
). Then, under

1
,
2
,
4
,
3
, and
SM1.1
,
if
\lVert\hat{\eta}-\eta_{0}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{n}
and
\overline{Z}_{n}\leq M_{n}
, then

R_{1i}\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\Delta_{n}^{2}M_{n}^{2}\log n.

Proof.

Observe that
R_{1i}\lesssim_{\sigma_{\ell},\sigma_{u},s_{0\ell},s_{0u}}\max\left({\Delta_{mi}^{2},\Delta_{si}^{2}}\right)\cdot\lVert H_{i}(\tilde{\eta}_{i},\hat{G}_{n})\rVert_{\infty}
,
where
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{\infty}
takes the largest element from a matrix by magnitude. By
assumption, the first term is bounded by
\Delta_{n}^{2}
. By
Lemma

SM1.11
,
the second derivatives are bounded by
M_{n}^{2}\log n
. Hence
\lVert H_{i}(\tilde{\eta}_{i},\hat{G}_{n})\rVert_{\infty}\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}M_{n}^{2}\log n
. This concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma SM1.6
.

Under
2
,
4
,
3
, and
SM1.1
, then

\operatorname{P}\left({\lVert\hat{\eta}-\eta_{0}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{n},\overline{Z}_{n}\leq M_{n},|R_{2}|\gtrsim_{\mathcal{H}}\Delta_{n}^{2}}\right)\leq\frac{1}{n}.

Proof.

Recall that
\mathbbm{1}(A_{n})=\mathbbm{1}(\lVert\hat{\eta}-\eta_{0}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{n},\overline{Z}_{n}\leq M_{n})
.
Note that

\mathbbm{1}(A_{n})|R_{2}|\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\Delta_{n}^{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}(A_{n})\lVert H_{i}\rVert_{\infty}.

by
(1,\infty)
-Hölder inequality. Moreover, note that the second derivatives((
SM1.11
), (
SM1.9
) , (
SM1.11
)) that occur in entries
of
H_{i}
are functions of posterior moments under
G_{0}
, evaluated at
Z_{i}=\hat{Z}_{i}(\hat{\eta}_{i})
. By

Lemma

SM1.14
,
under
G_{0},
these posterior moments are bounded above by corresponding moments of

\hat{Z}_{i}(\tilde{\eta}_{i}).
Hence,

\mathbbm{1}(A_{n})\lVert H_{i}\rVert_{\infty}\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\mathbbm{1}(A_{n})(\hat{Z}_{i}(\tilde{\eta}_{i})\vee 1)^{4}\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}(Z_{i}\vee 1)^{4}.

Hence,
\mathbbm{1}(A_{n})|R_{2}|\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\Delta_{n}^{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(Z_{i}\vee 1)^{4}.

Chebyshev’s inequality implies that there exists some choice
C_{\mathcal{H}}
such that

\operatorname{P}\left[{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(Z_{i}\vee 1)^{4}\geq C_{\mathcal{H}}}\right]\leq\frac{1}{n},

since
\operatorname{Var}(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(Z_{i}\vee 1)^{4})\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\frac{1}{n}
.
Hence,
\operatorname{P}\left({\lVert\hat{\eta}-\eta_{0}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{n},\overline{Z}_{n}\leq M_{n},|R_{2}|\gtrsim_{\mathcal{H}}\Delta_{n}^{2}}\right)\leq\frac{1}{n}.
∎

SM1.8.
Complexity of
\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R})
under moment-based distance

The following is a minor generalization of Lemma 4 and Theorem 7 in

Jiang 2020h
. In particular,
Jiang 2020h
’s Lemma 4
reduces to the case
q=0
below, and
Jiang 2020h
’s Theorem 7
relies on the results below for
q=0,1
. The proof largely
follows the proofs of these two results of
Jiang 2020h
.

We first state the following fact readily verified by differentiation.

Lemma SM1.7
.

For all integer
m\geq 0
:

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}|t^{m}\varphi(t)|=m^{m/2}\varphi(\sqrt{m}).

As a corollary, there exists absolute
C_{m}>0
such that
t\mapsto t^{m}\varphi(t)
is
C_{m}
-Lipschitz.

Proposition SM1.1
.

Fix some
q\in\mathbb{N}\cup\left\{{0}\right\}
and
M>1
.
Consider the pseudometric

d_{\infty,M}^{(q)}(G_{1},G_{2})=\max_{i\in[n]}\underbrace{\max_{0\leq v\leq q}\sup_{|x|\leq M}\bigg\lvert\int\frac{(u-x)^{v}}{\nu_{i}^{v}}\varphi\left({\frac{x-u}{\nu_{i}}}\right)(G_{1}-G_{2})(du)\bigg\rvert}_{d_{q,i,m}(G_{1},G_{2})}.

Let
\nu_{\ell},\nu_{u}
be the lower and upper bounds of
\nu_{i}
.
Then, for all
0<\delta<\exp(-q/2)\wedge e^{-1}
,

\log N(\delta\log^{q/2}(1/\delta),\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}),d_{\infty,M}^{(q)})\lesssim_{q,\nu_{u},\nu_{\ell}}\log^{2}(1/\delta)\max\left({\frac{M}{\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}},1}\right).

Proof.

The proof strategy is as follows.
First, we discretize
[-M,M]
into a
union of small intervals
I_{j}
. Fix
G
. There exists a finitely supported distribution

G_{m}
that matches moments of
G
on every
I_{j}
. It turns out that such a
G_{m}
is close
to
G
in terms of
d^{(q)}_{\infty,M}
. Next, we discipline
G_{m}
by
approximating
G_{m}
with
G_{m,\omega}
, a finitely supported distribution supported on the
fixed grid
\left\{{k\omega:k\in\mathbb{Z}}\right\}\cap[-M,M]
.
This shows that there exists a
G_{m,\omega}
with finite support on a grid that
approximates
G
in
d_{\infty,M}^{(q)}
.
Finally, the set of all
G_{m,\omega}
’s may
be approximated by a finite set of distributions, and we count the size of this finite
set.

SM1.8.1.
Approximating
G
with
G_{m}

First, let us fix some
\omega<\varphi(\sqrt{q})\wedge\varphi(1)
to be chosen.
Let
a=\frac{\nu_{u}}{\nu_{\ell}}\varphi_{+}(\omega)\geq 1
. Let
I_{j}=[-M+(j-2)a\nu_{\ell},-M+(j-1)a\nu_{\ell}]
be such that

I\equiv[-M-a\nu_{\ell},+M+a\nu_{\ell}]\subset\bigcup_{j=1}^{j^{*}}I_{j}

where
I_{j}
is a width
a\nu_{\ell}
interval. Let
j^{*}=\lceil\frac{2M}{a\nu_{\ell}}+2\rceil
be the number of such intervals.

For some
k^{*}
to be chosen, there exists by Carathéodory’s theorem a distribution
G_{m}

with support on
I
and no
more than

m=(2k^{*}+q+1)j^{*}+1

support points such that the moments up to
2k^{*}+q
match

\int_{I_{j}}u^{k}dG(u)=\int_{I_{j}}u^{k}dG_{m}(u)\text{ for all $k=0,\ldots,2k^{*}+q$ and
$j=1,\ldots,j^{*}$}.

Then, by analyzing
x\in I_{j}\cap[-M,M]
, we have that

(SM1.43)

\displaystyle d_{q,i,M}(G,G_{m})

\displaystyle\leq\max_{0\leq v\leq q}\max_{j=1,\ldots,j^{*}}\sup_{x\in I_{j}\cap[-M,M]}\Bigg[\bigg\lvert\int_{(I_{j-1}\cup I_{j}\cup I_{j+1})^{\mathrm{C}}}\left({\frac{u-x}{\nu_{i}}}\right)^{v}\varphi\left({\frac{x-u}{\nu_{i}}}\right)(G(du)-G_{m}(du))\bigg\rvert

(SM1.44)

\displaystyle+\bigg\lvert\int_{I_{j-1}\cup I_{j}\cup I_{j+1}}\left({\frac{u-x}{\nu_{i}}}\right)^{v}\varphi\left({\frac{x-u}{\nu_{i}}}\right)(G(du)-G_{m}(du))\bigg\rvert\Bigg]

Note that
t\mapsto|t|^{v}\varphi(t)

is a decreasing function in
|t|
, as long as
|t|>\sqrt{v}
. Note that over
u\not\in I_{j-1}\cup I_{j}\cup I_{j+1}
and
x\in I_{j}
,
\frac{|u-x|}{\nu_{i}}\geq a\nu_{\ell}/\nu_{u}=\varphi_{+}(\omega)\geq\sqrt{q}\geq\sqrt{v}
.
Hence,

\left|\left({\frac{u-x}{\nu_{i}}}\right)^{v}\varphi\left({\frac{x-u}{\nu_{i}}}\right)\right|\leq\varphi_{+}(\omega)^{q}\omega\implies\eqref{eq:outsideintervaldiff}\leq 2\varphi_{+}(\omega)^{q}\omega.

For (
SM1.44
), note that by the Taylor series for
e^{x}
,

\varphi(t)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-t^{2}/2)^{k}}{\sqrt{2\pi}k!}=\sum_{k=0}^{k^{*}}\frac{(-t^{2}/2)^{k}}{\sqrt{2\pi}k!}+R(t).

Thus the second term (
SM1.44
) can bounded by the maximum-over-
v
of the
absolute value of

\sum_{k=0}^{k^{*}}\int\frac{\left({\frac{x-u}{\nu_{i}}}\right)^{v+2k}(-1/2)^{k}}{\sqrt{2\pi}k!}[G(du)-G_{m}(du)]+\int R\left({\frac{x-u}{\nu_{i}}}\right)\left({\frac{x-u}{\nu_{i}}}\right)^{v}[G(du)-G_{m}(du)]

The first term in the line above is zero since the moments match up to
2k^{*}+q
.
Therefore,

\eqref{eq:intervaldiff}\leq\max_{0\leq v\leq q}\bigg\lvert\int_{I_{j-1}\cup I_{j}\cup I_{j+1}}\left({\frac{u-x}{\nu_{i}}}\right)^{v}R\left({\frac{x-u}{\nu_{i}}}\right)(G(du)-G_{m}(du))\bigg\rvert.

For
x\in I_{j}
, in (
SM1.44
),
|(x-u)/\nu_{i}|\leq 2a\nu_{\ell}/\nu_{i}\leq 2a
. On
[-2a,2a]
, if we choose
k^{*}>(2a)^{2}/2
, then
R(t)
is an infinite series with
alternating signs and decreasing entries. Thus,
R(t)

is bounded by the first term of truncation

|R(t)|\leq\frac{(t^{2}/2)^{k^{*}+1}}{\sqrt{2\pi}(k^{*}+1)!}\hskip 9.24994pt|t|\leq 2a.

Hence the integral
(
SM1.8.1
) is upper bounded by

(
(2a)^{v}\leq(2a)^{q}
)

(
SM1.44
)

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot(2a)^{q}\cdot\frac{\left({(2a)^{2}/2}\right)^{k^{*}+1}}{\sqrt{2\pi}(k^{*}+1)!}

(Stirling’s formula
(k^{*}+1)!\geq\sqrt{2\pi(k^{*}+1)}\left({\frac{k^{*}+1}{e}}\right)^{k^{*}+1}

)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2(2a)^{q}}{(2\pi)\sqrt{k^{*}+1}}\left({\frac{2a^{2}}{k^{*}+1}e}\right)^{k^{*}+1}

(Choosing
k^{*}+1\geq 6a^{2}\geq 6
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(2a)^{q}}{\pi\sqrt{k^{*}+1}}\left({\frac{e}{3}}\right)^{k^{*}+1}

(
(e/3)^{6}\leq e^{-1/2}
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(2a)^{q}}{\pi\sqrt{k^{*}+1}}\exp\left({-\frac{1}{2}\frac{k^{*}+1}{6}}\right)

(
k^{*}+1\geq 6a^{2}\geq 6(a\nu_{\ell}/\nu_{u})^{2}
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(2a)^{q}}{\sqrt{k^{*}+1}\sqrt{\pi/2}}\underbrace{\varphi(a\nu_{\ell}/\nu_{u})}_{\varphi(\varphi_{+}(\omega))}\leq\frac{(2a)^{q}}{\sqrt{k^{*}+1}\sqrt{\pi/2}}\omega

(
k^{*}+1\geq 6a^{2}
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{q}}{\sqrt{3\pi}}\left({\frac{\nu_{u}}{\nu_{\ell}}}\right)^{q-1}\varphi_{+}^{q-1}(\omega)\omega

These bounds
\eqref{eq:outsideintervaldiff}+\eqref{eq:intervaldiff}
since the bounds
do not depend on
j
or
x
. Therefore,

d_{q,i,M}(G,G_{m})\leq\left({2+\frac{2^{q}}{\sqrt{3\pi}}(\nu_{u}/\nu_{\ell})^{q-1}}\right)\cdot\varphi_{+}^{q}(\omega)\omega\lesssim_{q,\nu_{u},\nu_{\ell}}\log^{q/2}(1/\omega)\omega.

SM1.8.2.
Disciplining
G_{m}
onto a fixed grid

Now, consider a gridding of
G_{m}
via
G_{m,\omega}
. We construct
G_{m,\omega}
to be the
following distribution. For a draw
\xi\sim G_{m}
, let
\tilde{\xi}=\omega\operatorname{sgn}(\xi)\lfloor|\xi|/\omega\rfloor
. We let
G_{m,\omega}
be the distribution of
\tilde{\xi}
.
G_{m,\omega}
has at most
m=(2k^{*}+q+1)j^{*}+1
support points since
G_{m}

has at most that many, and all its support points are multiples of
\omega
.

Since

\int g(x,u)G_{m,\omega}(du)=\int g(x,\omega\operatorname{sgn}(u)\lfloor|u|/\omega\rfloor)\,G_{m}(du)

we have that

\bigg\lvert\int g(x,u)G_{m,\omega}(du)-\int g(x,u)G_{m}(du)\bigg\rvert\leq\int|g(x,\omega\operatorname{sgn}(u)\lfloor|u|/\omega\rfloor)-g(x,u)|\,G_{m}(du)

In the case of
g(x,u)=\left({(x-u)/\nu_{i}}\right)^{v}\varphi((x-u)/\nu_{i})
, this function is
Lipschitz by
Lemma

SM1.7
, we thus
have that,

d_{q,i,M}(G_{m},G_{m,\omega})\leq\int C_{q}\frac{\omega}{\nu_{i}}G_{m}(du)\lesssim_{\nu_{\ell},q}\omega.

So far, we have shown that there exists a distribution with at most
m

support points, supported on the lattice points
\left\{{j\omega:j\in\mathbb{Z},|j\omega|\in I}\right\}
, that approximates
G
up to

\omega^{*}\equiv C_{q,\nu_{u},\nu_{\ell}}\omega\log^{q/2}(1/\omega)

in
d_{\infty,M}^{(q)}(\cdot,\cdot)
.

SM1.8.3.
Covering the set of
G_{m,\omega}

Let
\Delta^{m-1}
be the
(m-1)
-simplex of probability vectors in
m
dimensions.
Consider discrete distributions supported on the support points of
G_{m,\omega}
, which
can be identified with a subset of
\Delta^{m-1}
. Thus, there are at most
N(\omega,\Delta^{m-1},\lVert\cdot\rVert_{1})
such distributions that form an
\omega
-net in

\lVert\cdot\rVert_{1}
. Now, consider a distribution
G_{m,\omega}^{\prime}
where

\lVert G_{m,\omega}^{\prime}-G_{m,\omega}\rVert_{1}\leq\omega.

Since
t^{q}\varphi(t)
is bounded, we have that

d_{q,i,M}(G_{m,\omega}^{\prime},G_{m,\omega})\leq\omega\max_{0\leq v\leq q}v^{v/2}\varphi(\sqrt{v})\lesssim_{q}\omega

by
Lemma

SM1.7
.

There are at most

\binom{1+2\lfloor(M+a\nu_{\ell})/\omega\rfloor}{m}

configurations of
m
support points. Hence there are a collection of at
most

\binom{1+2\lfloor(M+a\nu_{\ell})/\omega\rfloor}{m}N(\omega,\Delta^{m-1},\lVert\cdot\rVert_{1})

distributions
\mathcal{G}
where for all
G\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R})
,

\min_{H\in\mathcal{G}}d_{\infty,M}^{(q)}(G,H)\leq\omega^{*}.

SM1.8.4.
Putting things together

We have shown that

\displaystyle N(\omega^{*},\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}),d^{(q)}_{\infty,M})

\displaystyle\leq\binom{1+2\lfloor(M+a\nu_{\ell})/\omega\rfloor}{m}N(\omega,\Delta^{m-1},\lVert\cdot\rVert_{1})

((6.24) in
Jiang 2020h
)

\displaystyle\leq\left({\frac{(\omega+2)(\omega+2(M+a\nu_{\ell}))e}{m}}\right)^{m}\omega^{-2m}(2\pi m)^{-1/2}.

Since
\omega<1
and
m\geq 2\frac{12a^{2}+3+q}{a\nu_{\ell}}(M+a\nu_{\ell})
given
the choice
k^{*}+1>6a^{2}
, the first
term is bounded by a constant raised to
m
th
power:

\frac{(\omega+2)(\omega+2(M+a\nu_{\ell}))e}{m}\leq\frac{3e}{m}(1+2(M+a\nu_{\ell}))\lesssim\frac{a\nu_{\ell}}{12a^{2}+3+q}\lesssim\nu_{\ell}.

Therefore,

\log N(\omega^{*},\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}),d^{(q)}_{\infty,M})\lesssim_{\nu_{\ell},\nu_{u},q}m\cdot|\log(1/\omega)|+m\lesssim_{\nu_{\ell},\nu_{u},q}m\log(1/\omega).

Finally, since
m=(2k^{*}+q+1)j^{*}+1
, recall that we have required
k^{*}+1\geq 6a^{2}
, and it suffices to pick
k^{*}=\lceil 6a^{2}\rceil
. Then

m\lesssim_{q,\nu_{u},\nu_{\ell}}\log(1/\omega)\max\left({\frac{M}{\sqrt{\log(1/\omega)}},1}\right).

Hence,

\log N(\omega^{*},\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}),d^{(q)}_{\infty,M})\lesssim_{q,\nu_{u},\nu_{\ell}}\log(1/\omega)^{2}\max\left({\frac{M}{\sqrt{\log(1/\omega)}},1}\right).

Lastly, let
K
equal the constant in
\omega^{*}=K\log(1/\omega)^{q/2}\omega
. Note that
we can take
K\geq 1
. For some
c>1
such that
\log(cK)^{q/2}<c
, we plug in

\omega=\frac{\delta}{cK}

such that whenever
\delta<cK(\varphi(1)\wedge\varphi(\sqrt{q}))\wedge e^{-q/2}
,
the
covering number bound holds for

\omega^{*}=\frac{\delta}{c}\log(cK/\delta)^{q/2}\leq\delta\log(1/\delta)^{q/2}.

In this case,

\displaystyle\log N\left({\delta\log(1/\delta)^{q/2},\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}),d^{(q)}_{\infty,M}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\log N\left({\omega^{*},\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}),d^{(q)}_{\infty,M}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{q,\nu_{u},\nu_{\ell}}\log(1/\omega)^{2}\max\left({\frac{M}{\sqrt{\log(1/\omega)}},1}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{q,\nu_{u},\nu_{\ell}}\log(1/\delta)^{2}\max\left({\frac{M}{\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}},1}\right)

This bound holds for all sufficiently small
\delta
. Since
\delta\log(1/\delta)^{q/2}

is increasing over
(0,e^{-q/2}\wedge e^{-1})
and the right-hand side does not vanish
over the interval, we can absorb larger
\delta
’s into the constant.
∎

As a consequence, we can control the covering number in terms of
d_{k,\infty,M}
for

k\in\left\{{m,s}\right\}
.

Proposition SM1.2
.

Consider
d_{\infty,M}^{(q)}
in
SM1.1
and
d_{s,\infty,M}
and
d_{m,\infty,M}
in (
SM1.5
) for some
M>1
.
Suppose
d_{\infty,M}^{(2)}(H_{1},H_{2})\leq\delta
. Then we have

\displaystyle d_{m,\infty,M}(H_{1},H_{2})\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\frac{\sqrt{\log(1/\rho_{n})}}{\rho_{n}}\delta.

\displaystyle d_{s,\infty,M}(H_{1},H_{2})\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\frac{M\sqrt{\log(1/\rho_{n})}+\log(1/\rho_{n})}{\rho_{n}}\delta.

As a corollary, for all
\delta\in(0,1/e)
,

\displaystyle\log N\left({\frac{\delta\log(1/\delta)}{\rho_{n}}\sqrt{\log(1/\rho_{n})},\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}),d_{m,\infty,M}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\log(1/\delta)^{2}\max\left({1,\frac{M}{\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}}\right)

\displaystyle\log N\left({\frac{\delta\log(1/\delta)}{\rho_{n}}\left({M\sqrt{\log(1/\rho_{n})}+\log(1/\rho_{n})}\right),\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}),d_{s,\infty,M}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\log(1/\delta)^{2}\max\left({1,\frac{M}{\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}}\right).

Proof.

Fix some
|z|\leq M
. Let
k\in\left\{{m,s}\right\}
. Let
T_{mi}=f^{\prime}_{G,\nu_{i}}(z)
and
T_{si}=Q_{i}(z,\eta_{0},G)
. Observe that

\displaystyle|D_{k,i}(z,G_{1},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})-D_{k,i}(z,G_{2},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\left|\frac{T_{ki}(z,\eta_{0},G_{1})}{f_{G_{1},\nu_{i}}(z)\vee(\rho_{n}/\nu_{i})}-\frac{T_{ki}(z,\eta_{0},G_{2})}{f_{G_{2},\nu_{i}}(z)\vee(\rho_{n}/\nu_{i})}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\left|\frac{T_{ki}(z,\eta_{0},G_{1})}{f_{G_{1},\nu_{i}}(z)\vee(\rho_{n}/\nu_{i})}-\frac{T_{ki}(z,\eta_{0},G_{2})}{f_{G_{1},\nu_{i}}(z)\vee(\rho_{n}/\nu_{i})}+\frac{T_{ki}(z,\eta_{0},G_{2})}{f_{G_{1},\nu_{i}}(z)\vee(\rho_{n}/\nu_{i})}-\frac{T_{ki}(z,\eta_{0},G_{2})}{f_{G_{2},\nu_{i}}(z)\vee(\rho_{n}/\nu_{i})}\right|

(
|f_{1}\vee\rho-f_{2}\vee\rho|\leq|f_{1}-f_{2}|
)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\frac{1}{\rho_{n}}\lvert T_{ki}(z,\eta_{0},G_{1})-T_{ki}(z,\eta_{0},G_{2})\rvert+\frac{|T_{ki}(z,\eta_{0},G_{2})|}{\rho_{n}(f_{G_{2},\nu_{i}}(z)\vee(\rho_{n}/\nu_{i}))}|f_{G_{1},\nu_{i}}(z)-f_{G_{2},\nu_{i}}(z)|.

Now, if
d^{(2)}_{\infty,M}(G_{1},G_{2})\leq\delta
, then

\displaystyle|f_{G_{1},\nu_{i}}(z)-f_{G_{2},\nu_{i}}(z)|

\displaystyle\leq\delta

(
Lemma

SM1.8
)

\displaystyle\frac{\lvert T_{mi}(z,\eta_{0},G_{2})\rvert}{f_{G_{2},\nu_{i}}(z)\vee(\rho_{n}/\nu_{i})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\sqrt{\log(1/\rho_{n})}

(
Lemma

SM1.10
)

\displaystyle\frac{\lvert T_{si}(z,\eta_{0},G_{2})\rvert}{f_{G_{2},\nu_{i}}(z)\vee(\rho_{n}/\nu_{i})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}M\sqrt{\log(1/\rho_{n})}+\log(1/\rho_{n})

\displaystyle\lvert T_{mi}(z,\eta_{0},G_{1})-T_{mi}(z,\eta_{0},G_{2})\rvert

\displaystyle=\left|\int\frac{u-z}{\nu_{i}}\varphi\left({\frac{z-u}{\nu_{i}}}\right)(G_{1}-G_{2})(du)\right|\leq\delta

\displaystyle\lvert T_{si}(z,\eta_{0},G_{1})-T_{si}(z,\eta_{0},G_{2})\rvert

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\bigg\lvert\int\frac{\overbrace{(z-\tau)\tau}^{-(z-\tau)^{2}+z(z-\tau)}}{\nu_{i}^{2}}\varphi\left({\frac{z-\tau}{\nu_{i}}}\right)(G_{1}-G_{2})(du)\bigg\rvert\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}M\delta.

As a result,

\displaystyle|D_{m,i}(z,G_{1},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})-D_{m,i}(z,G_{2},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\frac{1}{\rho_{n}}\left[{\delta\sqrt{\log(1/\rho_{n})}}\right]

\displaystyle|D_{s,i}(z,G_{1},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})-D_{s,i}(z,G_{2},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\frac{\delta}{\rho_{n}}(M+M\sqrt{\log(1/\rho_{n})}+\log(1/\rho_{n}))

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\frac{\delta}{\rho_{n}}(M\sqrt{\log(1/\rho_{n})}+\log(1/\rho_{n})).

This proves the first claim.

For each
k\in\left\{{m,s}\right\}
, we can write

|D_{k,i}(z,G_{1},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})-D_{k,i}(z,G_{2},\eta_{0},\rho_{n})|\leq C_{\mathcal{H}}r_{kn}\delta.

for some
r_{kn}
and
C_{\mathcal{H}}>1
. Fix some
\kappa>0
and let
\delta=2C_{\mathcal{H}}\kappa
.
Then

\delta\log(1/\delta)=2C_{\mathcal{H}}\kappa\log(1/\kappa)-2C_{\mathcal{H}}\kappa\log(2C_{\mathcal{H}})

For all sufficiently small
\kappa
s.t.
\log(1/\kappa)>2\log(2C_{\mathcal{H}})
, the above is
bounded above by
C_{\mathcal{H}}\kappa\log(1/\kappa)
.
This immediately shows that

\displaystyle\log N(r_{kn}\delta\log(1/\delta),\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}),d_{k,\infty,M})

\displaystyle\leq\log N(r_{kn}C_{\mathcal{H}}\kappa\log(1/\kappa),\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}),d_{k,\infty,M})

\displaystyle\leq\log N(\kappa\log(1/\kappa),\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}),d^{(q)}_{\infty,M})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\log(1/\kappa)^{2}\max\left({\frac{M}{\sqrt{\log(1/\kappa)}},1}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\log(1/\delta)^{2}\max\left({\frac{M}{\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}},1}\right).

This holds for all sufficiently small
\delta
. Bounds for larger
\delta
can be
absorbed into the constant. Plugging in
r_{kn}
to the left-hand side concludes the
result.
∎

SM1.9.
Auxiliary lemmas

See
OA1.1

Proof.

For (1), observe that since
\hat{s},s_{0}
are bounded away from 0 and
\infty
under

4
,
|\hat{Z}_{i}|\vee 1\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}(1+\Delta_{n})M_{n}+\Delta_{n}\lesssim(1+\Delta_{n})M_{n}
. Hence by
SM1.1
,
|\hat{Z}_{i}|\vee 1\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}M_{n}
.

For (2), we note by Theorem 5 in
Jiang 2020h
,

f_{\hat{G}_{n},\hat{\nu}_{i}}(\hat{Z}_{i})\geq\frac{1}{n^{3}\hat{\nu}_{i}}

thanks to the choice
\kappa_{n}
in
1
. That is,

\int\varphi\left({\frac{\hat{Z}_{i}-\tau}{\hat{\nu}_{i}}}\right)\,\hat{G}_{n}(d\tau)\geq\frac{1}{n^{3}}.

Now, note that

\frac{\hat{Z}_{i}-\tau}{\hat{\nu}_{i}}=\frac{Z_{i}-\tau}{\nu_{i}}+\frac{m_{0i}-\hat{m}_{i}}{\sigma_{i}}+\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}}(\hat{s}_{i}-s_{0i})\tau\equiv\frac{Z_{i}-\tau}{\nu_{i}}+\xi(\tau)

where
|\xi(\tau)|\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\Delta_{n}M_{n}
over the support of
\tau
under
\hat{G}_{n}
, under
our assumptions.

Then, for all
Z_{i}
, since
|Z_{i}|\leq M_{n}
by assumption,

\displaystyle\varphi\left({\frac{\hat{Z}_{i}-\tau}{\hat{\nu}_{i}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\varphi\left({\frac{Z_{i}-\tau}{\nu_{i}}}\right)\exp\left({-\frac{1}{2}\xi^{2}(\tau)-\xi(\tau)\frac{Z_{i}-\tau}{\nu_{i}}}\right)

(
C_{\mathcal{H}}
is defined by optimizing over
|\xi(\tau)|\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\Delta_{n}M_{n}
)

\displaystyle\leq\varphi\left({\frac{Z_{i}-\tau}{\nu_{i}}}\right)\exp\left({C_{\mathcal{H}}\Delta_{n}M_{n}\left|\frac{Z_{i}-\tau}{\nu_{i}}\right|}\right)

(
\left|\frac{Z_{i}-\tau}{\nu_{i}}\right|\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}M_{n}
)

\displaystyle\leq\varphi\left({\frac{Z_{i}-\tau}{\nu_{i}}}\right)\exp\left({C_{\mathcal{H}}\Delta_{n}M_{n}^{2}}\right).

Therefore,

\int\varphi\left({\frac{Z_{i}-\tau}{\nu_{i}}}\right)\hat{G}_{n}(d\tau)\geq\frac{1}{n^{3}}e^{-C_{\mathcal{H}}\Delta_{n}M_{n}^{2}}.

Dividing by
\nu_{i}
on both sides finishes the proof of (2).
Claim (3) is immediate by calculating
\log(1/\rho_{n})=\left({3\log n+C_{\mathcal{H}}M_{n}^{2}\Delta_{n}}\right)\vee\log(e\sqrt{2\pi})\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\log n
and applying
SM1.1
(1) to
obtain that
\Delta_{n}M_{n}^{2}\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}1
.
∎

Lemma SM1.8

(Lemma 2,
Jiang 2020h
)
.

For all
x\in\mathbb{R}
and all
\rho\in(0,1/\sqrt{2\pi e})
,

\bigg\lvert\frac{\nu^{2}f^{\prime}_{H,\nu}(x)}{(\rho/\nu)\vee f_{H,\nu}(x)}\bigg\rvert\leq\nu\varphi_{+}(\rho).

Moreover, for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
and all
\rho\in(0,e^{-1}/\sqrt{2\pi}),

\bigg\lvert\left({\frac{\nu^{2}f^{\prime\prime}_{H,\nu}(x)}{f_{H,\nu}(z)}+1}\right)\left({\frac{\nu f_{H,\nu}(x)}{(\nu f_{G,\nu}(x))\vee\rho}}\right)\bigg\rvert\leq\varphi_{+}^{2}(\rho),

where we recall
\varphi_{+}(\rho)=\sqrt{\log\frac{1}{2\pi\rho^{2}}}
from
(
OA1.3
).

Proof.

The first claim is immediate from Lemma 2 in
Jiang 2020h
.
The second claim follows from parts of the proof, which we reproduce here. Lemma 1 in

Jiang 2020h

shows that

0\leq\frac{\nu^{2}f^{\prime\prime}_{H,\nu}(x)}{f_{H,\nu}(z)}+1\leq{\log\frac{1}{2\pi\nu^{2}f_{H,\nu}(z)^{2}}}={\varphi_{+}^{2}(\nu f_{H,\nu}(z))}.

We study two cases separetely depending on whether the truncation binds:

(1)

\nu f_{H,\nu}(x)\leq\rho<e^{-1}/\sqrt{2\pi}
: Observe that
t\log\frac{1}{2\pi t^{2}}
is
increasing over
t\in(0,e^{-1}(2\pi)^{-1/2})
. Hence,

\left({\frac{\nu^{2}f^{\prime\prime}_{H,\nu}(x)}{f_{H,\nu}(z)}+1}\right)\nu f_{H,\nu}(x)\leq\nu f_{H,\nu}\log\frac{1}{2\pi\nu^{2}f_{H,\nu}(z)^{2}}\leq\rho\log\frac{1}{2\pi\rho^{2}}=\rho\varphi_{+}^{2}(\rho).

Dividing by
(\nu f)\vee\rho=\rho
confirms the bound for
\nu f<\rho
.

(2)

\nu f_{H,\nu}(x)>\rho
: Since
\log\frac{1}{2\pi t^{2}}
is decreasing in
t
, we have
that

\bigg\lvert\left({\frac{\nu^{2}f^{\prime\prime}_{H,\nu}(x)}{f_{H,\nu}(z)}+1}\right)\left({\frac{\nu f_{H,\nu}(x)}{(\nu f_{G,\nu}(x))\vee\rho}}\right)\bigg\rvert={\frac{\nu^{2}f^{\prime\prime}_{H,\nu}(x)}{f_{H,\nu}(z)}+1}\leq\varphi_{+}^{2}(\nu f_{H,\nu})\leq\log\frac{1}{2\pi\rho^{2}}=\varphi_{+}^{2}(\rho).\qed

Lemma SM1.9

(
Zhang 1997b
, p.186)
.

Let
f
be a density and let
\sigma(f)
be the standard deviation of the corresponding
distribution, assumed to be finite. Then, for any
M,t>0
,

\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathbbm{1}(f(z)\leq t)f(z)\,dz\leq\frac{\sigma(f)^{2}}{M^{2}}+2Mt.

In particular, choosing
M=t^{-1/3}\sigma(f)^{2/3}
gives

\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathbbm{1}(f(z)\leq t)f(z)\,dz\leq 3t^{2/3}\sigma^{2/3}.

Proof.

Since the value of the integral does not change if we shift
f(z)
to
f(z-c)
, it is
without loss of generality to assume that
\mathbb{E}_{f}[Z]=0
.

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathbbm{1}(f(z)\leq t)f(z)\,dz

\displaystyle\leq\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathbbm{1}(f(z)\leq t,|z|<M)f(z)\,dz+\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathbbm{1}(f(z)\leq t,|z|>M)f(z)\,dz

\displaystyle\leq\int_{-M}^{M}t\,dz+\operatorname{P}(|Z|>M)

(Chebyshev’s inequality)

\displaystyle\leq 2Mt+\frac{\sigma^{2}(f)}{M^{2}}.

∎

Lemma SM1.10
.

Recall that
Q_{i}(z,\eta,G)=\int(z-\tau)\tau\varphi\left({\frac{z-\tau}{\nu_{i}(\eta)}}\right)\frac{1}{\nu_{i}(\eta)}\,G(d\tau)
in (
SM1.5
). Then,
for any
G,z
and
\rho_{n}\in(0,e^{-1}/\sqrt{2\pi})
,

(SM1.47)

\bigg\lvert\frac{Q_{i}(z,\eta_{0},G)}{f_{G,\nu_{i}}(z)\vee(\rho_{n}/\nu_{i})}\bigg\rvert\leq\varphi_{+}(\rho_{n})\nu_{i}\left({|z|+\nu_{i}\varphi_{+}(\rho_{n})}\right).

Under the choice (
OA1
) and on the event
\overline{Z}_{n}\leq M_{n}
such that

SM1.1
holds,

\bigg\lvert\frac{Q_{i}(z,\eta_{0},G)}{f_{G,\nu_{i}}(z)\vee(\rho_{n}/\nu_{i})}\bigg\rvert\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}M_{n}\sqrt{\log n}.

Proof.

We can write

Q_{i}(z,\eta_{0},G)=f_{G,\nu_{i}}(z)\left\{{z\mathbf{E}_{G,\nu_{i}}[(z-\tau)\mid z]-\mathbf{E}_{G_{n},\nu_{i}}[(z-\tau)^{2}\mid z]}\right\}.

From
Lemma

SM1.8
,

\left|\frac{f_{G,\nu_{i}}(z)}{f_{G,\nu_{i}}(z)\vee(\rho_{n}/\nu_{i})}\mathbf{E}_{G,\nu_{i}}[(z-\tau)\mid z]\right|\leq\nu_{i}\varphi_{+}(\rho_{n})

and

\frac{f_{G,\nu_{i}}(z)}{f_{G,\nu_{i}}(z)\vee(\rho_{n}/\nu_{i})}\mathbf{E}_{G,\nu_{i}}[(z-\tau)^{2}\mid z]=\nu_{i}^{2}\left({\frac{\nu_{i}^{2}f_{i,G}^{\prime\prime}}{f_{i,G}}+1}\right)\frac{f_{G,\nu_{i}}(z)}{f_{G,\nu_{i}}(z)\vee(\rho_{n}/\nu_{i})}\leq\nu_{i}^{2}\varphi_{+}^{2}(\rho_{n}).

Therefore,

\bigg\lvert\frac{Q_{i}(z,\eta_{0},G)}{f_{G,\nu_{i}}(z)\vee(\rho_{n}/\nu_{i})}\bigg\rvert\leq\varphi_{+}(\rho_{n})\nu_{i}\left({|z|+\nu_{i}\varphi_{+}(\rho_{n})}\right).\qed

Lemma SM1.11
.

Under the assumptions in
Lemma

OA1.1
and
4
, suppose
\tilde{\eta}_{i}

lies on the line segment between
\eta_{0}
and
\hat{\eta}_{i}
and define
\tilde{\nu}_{i},\tilde{m}_{i},\tilde{s}_{i},\tilde{Z}_{i}
accordingly. Then, the
second

), (

\displaystyle|\eqref{eq:d2psidm2}|\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}\log n\hskip 9.24994pt|\eqref{eq:cross_psi_deriv}|\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}M_{n}\log n\hskip 9.24994pt|\eqref{eq:d2psids2}|\lesssim_{\mathcal{H}}M_{n}^{2}\log n.

\hat{Z}_{i}=\frac{\tilde{s}_{i}\tilde{Z}_{i}+\tilde{m}_{i}-\hat{m}_{i}}{\hat{s}_{i}}
